  98→}\n    99→\n   100→/// Errors that may occur at the pipeline level.\n   101→#[derive(thiserror::Error, Debug)]\n   102→pub enum PipelineError {\n   103→    #[error(\"source kafka error: {0}\")]\n 177→/// The filter gives an O(1) approximate membership test with controllable\n   178→/// false-positive rate.  We complement it with an exact check in the\n   179→/// concurrent `DashSet` to eliminate false positives at the cost of\n   180→/// additional in-RAM storage for the most recent `DEDUP_CAPACITY` IDs.\n   181→pub struct DuplicateDetector {\n   182→    // Fast, memory-efficient approximate set.\n   183→    bloom: RwLock<BloomFilter>,\n   184→    // Slow but exact set of recent IDs. Mark in Bloom filter and exact set.\n   211→                drop(bloom);\n   212→                {\n   213→                    let mut bloom = self.bloom.write().await;\n   214→                    bloom.insert(id);\n   215→                }\n   216→                self.exact.insert(id.clone());\n   217→                return Ok(());\n   218→            }\n   219→        }\n   220→        // Bloom filter says \"maybe\". Perform exact check.\n   221→        if self.exact.contains(id) {\n   222→            Err(QualityError::Duplicate)\n   223→        } else {\n   224→            // False positive, record now.\n   225→            {\n   226→                let mut bloom = self.bloom.write().await;\n   227→                bloom.insert(id);\n   228→            }\n   229→            // Evict if over capacity.\n   230→            if self.exact.len() >= DEDUP_CAPACITY {\n   231→                if let Some(first) = self.exact.iter().next() {\n   232→                    self.exact.remove(first.key());\n   233→                }\n   234→            }\n   235→            self.exact.insert(id.clone());\n   236→            Ok(())\n   237→        }\n   238→    }\n   239→}\n   240→\n   241→//\n   242→// ──────────────────────────── Event Source ─────────────────────────────────────\n   243→//\n   244→\n   245→/// Convert a borrowed Kafka message to `SocialEvent`.\n   246→fn deserialize_event(msg: &BorrowedMessage<'_>) -> Result<SocialEvent, serde_json::Error> {\n   247→    serde_json::from_slice::<SocialEvent>(msg.payload().unwrap_or_default())\n   248→}\n   249→\n   250→/// Wrapper around a `StreamConsumer` that yields deserialized `SocialEvent`s.\n   251→fn kafka_event_stream<'a>(\n   252→    consumer: &'a StreamConsumer,\n   253→) -> impl Stream<Item = Result<SocialEvent, PipelineError>> + 'a {\n   254→    consumer\n   255→        .stream()\n   256→        .map(|result| match result {\n   257→            Err(e) => Err(PipelineError::Source(e.to_string())),\n   258→            Ok(m) => deserialize_event(&m).map_err(PipelineError::from),\n   259→        })\n   260→}\n   261→\n   262→//\n   263→// ───────────────────────────── Event Sink ──────────────────────────────────────\n   264→//\n   265→\n   266→/// Async sink that pushes JSON serialised events to a Kafka topic.\n   267→struct KafkaJsonSink {\n   268→    producer: FutureProducer,\n   269→    topic: &'static str,\n   270→}\n   271→\n   272→impl KafkaJsonSink {\n   273→    fn new(brokers: &str, topic: &'static str) -> anyhow::Result<Self> {\n   274→        let .create()?;\n   278→        Ok(Self { producer, topic })\n   279→    }\n   280→\n   281→    #[instrument(skip_all, fields(topic = self.topic))]\n   282→    async fn send(&self, event: &SocialEvent) -> Result<(), SinkError> {\n   283→        let payload = serde_json::to_vec(event)?;\n   284→        let record = FutureRecord::to(self.topic)\n   285→            .key(&event.event_id)\n   286→            .payload(&payload);\n   287→        let produce_future = self.producer.send(record, Duration::from_millis(PRODUCER_TIMEOUT_MS));\n   288→\n   289→        match produce_future.await {\n   290→            Ok(Ok(_)) => Ok(()),\n   291→            Ok(Err((e, _))) | Err((e, _)) => Err(SinkError::Produce(e.to_string())),\n   292→        }\n   293→    }\n   294→}\n   295→\n   296→//\n   297→// ───────────────────────────── Pipeline  ───────────────────────────────────────\n   298→//\n   299→\n   300→/// Thread-safe, hot-swapable registry of `QualityRule`s.\n   301→///\n   302→/// Data  306→    inner: RwLock<Vec<Arc<dyn QualityRule>>>,\n   307→}\n   308→\n   309→impl RuleRegistry {\n   310→    pub fn new(initial: Vec<Arc<dyn QualityRule>>) -> Self {\n   311→        Self {\n   312→            inner: RwLock::new(initial),\n   313→        }\n   314→    }\n   315→\n   316→    /// Atomically replace the full ruleset.\n   317→    pub async fn replace_rules(&self, rules: Vec<Arc<dyn QualityRule>>) {\n   318→        let mut guard = self.inner.write().await;\n   319→        *guard = rules;\n   320→        info!(count = guard.len(), \"Replaced quality-ruleset\");\n   321→    }\n }\n   327→}\n   328→\n   329→/// The main orchestrator that wires sources, rules and sink.\n   330→pub struct QualityPipeline {\n   331→    rules: Arc<RuleRegistry>,\n   332→    sink: .duration_since(start)\n   385→                                        .unwrap_or_default()\n   386→                                        .as_millis() as f64;\n   387→                                    histogram!(\"pipeline_event_latency_ms\", latency);\n   388→                                }\n   389→                                Err(e) => {\n   390→                                    error!(error = %e, \"Processing error\");\n   391→                                    counter!(\"pipeline_errors_total\", 1, \"stage\" => \"processing\");\n   392→                                    // Push to DLQ\n   393→                                    if let Err(dlq_err) = self.sink_dead_letter(&event, &e).await {\n   394→                                        error!(error = %dlq_err, \"Failed to write to DLQ\");\n   395→                                        counter!(\"pipeline_errors_total\", 1, \"stage\" => \"dlq\");\n   396→                                    }\n   397→                                }\n   398→                            }\n   399→                        }\n   400→                    }\n   401→                }\n   402→            }\n   403→        }\n .producer\n   443→            .send(record, Duration::from_millis(PRODUCER_TIMEOUT_MS))\n   444→            .await\n   445→        {\n   446→            Ok(Ok(_)) => Ok(()),\n   447→ We complement it with an exact check in the\n/// concurrent `DashSet` to eliminate false positives at the cost of\n/// additional in-RAM storage for the most recent `DEDUP_CAPACITY` IDs.\npub struct DuplicateDetector {\n    // Fast, memory-efficient approximate set.\n    bloom: RwLock<BloomFilter>,\n    // Slow but exact set of recent IDs.  Evicted by size limits.\n    exact: DashSet<String>,\n}\n\nimpl DuplicateDetector {\n    pub fn new(capacity: usize, fp_rate: f64) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            bloom: RwLock::new(BloomFilter::with_rate(fp_rate, capacity)),\n            exact: DashSet::with_capacity(capacity),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl QualityRule for DuplicateDetector {\n    fn name(&self) -> &'static str {\n        \"duplicate_detector\"\n    }\n\n    async fn check(&self, event: &mut SocialEvent) -> Result<(), QualityError> {\n        let id = &event.event_id;\n\n        // First, try the Bloom filter (approximate).\n        {\n            let bloom = self.bloom.read().await;\n            if !bloom.contains(id) {\n                // Definitely not seen before.  Mark in Bloom filter and exact set.\n                drop(bloom);\n                {\n                    let mut bloom = self.bloom.write().await;\n                    bloom.insert(id);\n                }\n                self.exact.insert(id.clone());\n                return Ok(());\n            }\n        }\n        // Bloom filter says \"maybe\". Perform exact check.\n        if self.exact.contains(id) {\n            Err(QualityError::Duplicate)\n        } else {\n            // False positive, record now.\n            {\n                let mut bloom = self.bloom.write().await;\n                bloom.insert(id);\n            }\n            // Evict if over capacity.\n            if self.exact.len() >= DEDUP_CAPACITY {\n                if let Some(first) = self.exact.iter().next() {\n                    self.exact.remove(first.key());\n                }\n            }\n            self.exact.insert(id.clone());\n            Ok(())\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n//\n// ──────────────────────────── Event Source ─────────────────────────────────────\n//\n\n/// Convert a borrowed Kafka message to `SocialEvent`.\nfn deserialize_event(msg: &BorrowedMessage<'_>) -> Result<SocialEvent, serde_json::Error> {\n    serde_json::from_slice::<SocialEvent>(msg.payload().unwrap_or_default())\n}\n\n/// Wrapper around a `StreamConsumer` that yields deserialized `SocialEvent`s.\nfn kafka_event_stream<'a>(\n    consumer: &'a StreamConsumer,\n) -> impl Stream<Item = Result<SocialEvent, PipelineError>> + 'a {\n    consumer\n        .stream()\n        .map(|result| match result {\n            Err(e) => Err(PipelineError::Source(e.to_string())),\n            Ok(m) => deserialize_event(&m).map_err(PipelineError::from),\n        })\n}\n\n//\n// ───────────────────────────── Event Sink ──────────────────────────────────────\n//\n\n/// Async sink that pushes JSON serialised events to a Kafka topic.\nstruct KafkaJsonSink {\n    producer: FutureProducer,\n    topic: &'static str,\n}\n\nimpl KafkaJsonSink {\n    fn new(brokers: &str, topic: &'static str) -> anyhow::Result<Self> {\n        let producer = ClientConfig::new()\n            .set(\"bootstrap.servers\", brokers)\n            .set(\"message.timeout.ms\", PRODUCER_TIMEOUT_MS.to_string())\n            .create()?;\n        Ok(Self { producer, topic })\n    }\n\n    #[instrument(skip_all, fields(topic = self.topic))]\n    async fn send(&self, event: &SocialEvent) -> Result<(), SinkError> {\n        let payload = serde_json::to_vec(event)?;\n        let record = FutureRecord::to(self.topic)\n            .key(&event.event_id)\n            .payload(&payload);\n        let produce_future = self.producer.send(record, Duration::from_millis(PRODUCER_TIMEOUT_MS));\n\n        match produce_future.await {\n            Ok(Ok(_)) => Ok(()),\n            Ok(Err((e, _))) | Err((e, _)) => Err(SinkError::Produce(e.to_string())),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n//\n// ───────────────────────────── Pipeline  ───────────────────────────────────────\n//\n\n/// Thread-safe, hot-swapable registry of `QualityRule`s.\n///\n/// Data scientists can update the active ruleset at run-time by calling\n/// [`RuleRegistry::replace_rules`] with a freshly built vector.\n#[derive(Default)]\npub struct RuleRegistry {\n    inner: RwLock<Vec<Arc<dyn QualityRule>>>,\n}\n\nimpl RuleRegistry {\n    pub fn new(initial: Vec<Arc<dyn QualityRule>>) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            inner: RwLock::new(initial),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Atomically replace the full ruleset.\n    pub async fn replace_rules(&self, rules: Vec<Arc<dyn QualityRule>>) {\n        let mut guard = self.inner.write().await;\n        *guard = rules;\n        info!(count = guard.len(), \"Replaced quality-ruleset\");\n    }\n\n    /// Fetch a snapshot clone of the current rules.\n    pub async fn snapshot(&self) -> Vec<Arc<dyn QualityRule>> {\n        self.inner.read().await.clone()\n    }\n}\n\n/// The main orchestrator that wires sources, rules and sink.\npub struct QualityPipeline {\n    rules: Arc<RuleRegistry>,\n    sink: Arc<KafkaJsonSink>,\n    cancel: Arc<RwLock<bool>>,\n}\n\nimpl QualityPipeline {\n    pub fn new(rules: Arc<RuleRegistry>, sink: Arc<KafkaJsonSink>) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            rules,\n            sink,\n            cancel: Arc::new(RwLock::new(false)),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Request graceful shutdown.\n    pub async fn shutdown(&self) {\n        let mut guard = self.cancel.write().await;\n        *guard = true;\n    }\n\n    /// Consumes from `consumer` indefinitely until `shutdown` is called.\n    #[instrument(skip_all, name = \"quality_pipeline\")]\n    pub async fn run(&self, consumer: StreamConsumer) -> anyhow::Result<()> {\n        info!(\"QC Pipeline starting\");\n        // Subscribe to topic.\n        consumer.subscribe(&[SOURCE_TOPIC])?;\n\n        // Stream loop with back-pressure.\n        let mut stream = kafka_event_stream(&consumer);\n\n        loop {\n            select! {\n                cancelled = self.cancel.read(), if *cancelled => {\n                    info!(\"QC Pipeline received shutdown signal\");\n                    break;\n                }\n                maybe_msg = stream.next() => {\n                    match maybe_msg {\n                        None => {\n                            warn!(\"Event stream unexpectedly ended\");\n                            break;\n                        }\n                        Some(Err(err)) => {\n                            error!(error = %err, \"Event stream error\");\n                            counter!(\"pipeline_errors_total\", 1, \"stage\" => \"source\");\n                        }\n                        Some(Ok(mut event)) => {\n                            // Process one event\n    }\n                            }\n                        }\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n        info!(\"QC Pipeline stopped\");\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    #[instrument(skip_all)]\n    async fn process_event(&self, event: &mut SocialEvent) -> Result<(), PipelineError> {\n        let rules = self.rules.snapshot().await;\n\n        for rule in rules.iter() {\n            // For every rule we catch its error separately to emit per-rule metrics.\n            match rule.check(event).await {\n                Ok(()) => #[instrument(skip_all)]\n    async fn sink_dead_letter(\n        &self,\n        event: &SocialEvent,\n        err: &PipelineError,\n    ) -> Result<(), SinkError> {\n        let payload = .key(&event.event_id)\n            .payload(&serde_json::to_vec(&payload)?);\n\n        match dlq_sink\n            .producer\n            .send(record, Duration::from_millis(PRODUCER_TIMEOUT_MS))\n            .await\n        {\n            Ok(Ok(_)) => Ok(()),\n            Ok(Err((e, _))) | Err((e, _)) => Err(SinkError::Produce(e.to_string())),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n//\n// .duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)\n            .unwrap_or_default()\n            .as_millis() as u64;\n        Self {\n            event,\n            error: error.to_string(),\n            failed_at,\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n//\n// ──────────────────────────────── main  ────────────────────────────────────────\n//\n\n#[tokio::main]\nasync fn main() -> anyhow::Result<()> {\n    // Initialise tracing and Part of: ChirpPulse – Real-Time Social Sentiment Lake\n//!\n//! Responsibility\n//! --------------\n//! Reads raw social events from Kafka, applies a configurable set of\n//! data-quality rules (missing language, invalid timestamps, etc.) and\n//! republishes the message to either\n//!   * `<stream>.qc.pass`  – when all rules succeed, or\n//!   * `<stream>.qc.fail`  – on the first rule failure.\n//!\n//! In addition, the service exposes Prometheus metrics for observability and\n//! gracefully handles re-balancing, back-pressure, and shutdown signals.\n//\n//  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n//  Production dependencies (in Cargo.toml)\n//  ======================================\n//  [dependencies]\n//  anyhow          = \"1\"\n//  chrono          = { version = \"0.4\", features = [\"serde\"] }\n//  futures         = \"0.3\"\n//  log             = \"0.4\"\n//  prometheus      = \"0.14\"\n//  rdkafka         = { ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── Data structures ─\n//\n\n/// Incoming raw social event\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Deserialize)]\n#[serde(rename_all = \"camelCase\")]\npub struct SocialEvent {\n    pub id: String,\n    pub stream: String, // e.g. \"twitter\", \"reddit\"\n    pub language: Option<String>,\n    pub created_at: DateTime<Utc>,\n    pub sentiment: Option<f32>,\n    pub payload: HashMap<String, serde_json::Value>,\n}\n\n/// Enriched event tagged with QC metadata\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize)]\n#[serde(rename_all = \"camelCase\")]\npub struct QualifiedEvent {\n    pub original: SocialEvent,\n    pub qc_passed: bool,\n    pub failed_rule: Option<String>,\n}\n\n/// Data-quality validation failure\n#[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]\npub enum QualityError {\n    #[error(\"missing field `{0}`\")]\n    MissingField(&'static str),\n    #[error(\"timestamp is in the future\")]\n    InvalidTimestamp,\n    #[error(\"sentiment score out of bounds: {0}\")]\n    SentimentOutOfBounds(f32),\n}\n\n/// Strategy trait for data-quality rules\npub trait DataQualityRule: Send + Sync {\n    /// Unique, stable name (used in metrics and failure payload)\n    fn name(&self) -> &'static str;\n\n    /// Returns `Ok(())` when the rule passes\n    fn check(&self, event: &SocialEvent) -> Result<(), QualityError>;\n}\n\ntype DynRule = Box<dyn DataQualityRule>;\n\n/// Ensure the language code is present\npub struct LanguagePresentRule;\n\nimpl DataQualityRule for LanguagePresentRule {\n    fn name(&self) -> &'static str {\n        \"language_present\"\n    }\n\n    fn check(&self, event: &SocialEvent) -> Result<(), QualityError> {\n        if event.language.is_some() {\n            Ok(())\n        } else {\n            Err(QualityError::MissingField(\"language\"))\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Ensure `created_at` <= *now* (+ 5s tolerance)\npub struct TimestampNotFutureRule;\n\nimpl DataQualityRule for TimestampNotFutureRule {\n    fn name(&self) -> &'static str {\n        \"timestamp_not_future\"\n    }\n\n    fn check(&self, event: &SocialEvent) -> Result<(), QualityError> {\n        let now = Utc::now() + chrono::Duration::seconds(5);\n        if event.created_at <= now {\n            Ok(())\n        } else {\n            Err(QualityError::InvalidTimestamp)\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Ensure sentiment ∈ [-1.0, 1.0] when present\npub struct SentimentBoundsRule;\n\nimpl DataQualityRule for SentimentBoundsRule {\n    fn name(&self) -> &'static str {\n        \"sentiment_bounds\"\n    }\n\n    fn check(&self, event: &SocialEvent) -> Result<(), QualityError> {\n        if let Some(s) = event.sentiment {\n            if s >= -1.0 && s <= 1.0 {\n                Ok(())\n            } else {\n                Err(QualityError::SentimentOutOfBounds(s))\n            }\n        } else {\n            Ok(()) // Absence is allowed here; another rule can enforce presence\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n// ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── Configuration ─────\n\n/// Service configuration loaded from env-vars\n#[derive(Debug, Clone)]\npub struct Config {\n    pub brokers: String,\n    pub group_id: String,\n    pub input_topic: String,\n    pub output_pass_topic: String,\n    pub \"localhost:9092\".into()),\n            group_id: std::env::var(\"CP_QC_GROUP\").unwrap_or_else(|_| \"qc-validator\".into()),\n            input_topic: ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── Metrics ───────────\n\n#[derive(Clone)]\nstruct Metrics {\n    registry: Registry,\n    processed: IntCounterVec,\n    latency: HistogramVec,\n}\n\nimpl Metrics {\n    fn new() -> Self {\n        let registry = Registry::new();\n\n        let processed = IntCounterVec::new(\n            prometheus::Opts::new(\"events_processed\", \"Total processed events\"),\n            &[\"result\"],\n        )\n        .expect(\"metric can be created\");\n        registry\n            .register(Box::new(processed.clone()))\n            .expect(\"metric can be registered\");\n\n        let latency = HistogramVec::new(\n            HistogramOpts::new(\"qc_latency_seconds\", \"QC processing latency\"),\n            &[\"result\"],\n        )\n        .expect(\"metric can be created\");\n\n        registry\n            .register(Box::new(latency.clone()))\n            .expect(\"metric can be registered\");\n\n        Self {\n            registry,\n            processed,\n            latency,\n        }\n    }\n\n    async fn serve(self: Arc<Self>) -> Result<()> {\n        let addr = ([0, 0, 0, 0], 9888).into();\n        let make_svc = hyper::service::make_service_fn(move |_| {\n            let m = self.clone();\n            async move {\n                Ok::<_, hyper::Error>(hyper::service::service_fn(move |_req| {\n                    let m = m.clone();\n                    async move {\n                        let encoder = TextEncoder::new();\n                        let mf = m.registry.gather();\n                        let mut buf = Vec::new();\n                        encoder.encode(&mf, &mut buf).unwrap();\n                        Ok::<_, }))\n            }\n        });\n        let server = hyper::Server::bind(&addr).serve(make_svc);\n        info!(\"Prometheus exporter listening on {}\", addr);\n\n        server.await?;\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── QC Engine ────────\n\n/// Central controller running all QC rules\npub struct QualityController {\n    rules: -> Self {\n        let rules: Vec<DynRule> = vec![\n            Box::new(LanguagePresentRule),\n            Box::new(TimestampNotFutureRule),\n            Box::new(SentimentBoundsRule),\n        ];\n        Self {\n            rules,\n            metrics,\n            producer,\n            cfg,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Apply all rules and emit results\n    async fn handle_message(&self, msg: BorrowedMessage<'_>) -> Result<()> {\n        let span = span!(Level::DEBUG, \"handle_message\");\n        async move {\n            let payload = match msg.payload_view::<str>() {\n                Some(Ok(v)) => v,\n                Some(Err(e)) => {\n                    error!(\"Invalid UTF-8 payload: {e}\");\n                    return Ok(());\n                }\n                None => {\n                    warn!(\"Received message with empty payload\");\n                    return Ok(());\n                }\n            };\n\n            let event: SocialEvent = match serde_json::from_str(payload) {\n                Ok(e) => e,\n                Err(e) => {\n                    warn!(\"Failed to deserialize social event: {e}\");\n                    return Ok(());\n                }\n            };\n\n            let timer = self\n                .metrics\n                .latency\n                .with_label_values(&[\"total\"])\n                .start_timer();\n\n            // Iterate rules\n            for rule in &self.rules {\n                if let Err(err) = rule.check(&event) {\n                    self.metrics\n                        .processed\n                        .with_label_values(&[\"fail\"])\n                        .inc();\n                    self.metrics\n                        .latency\n                        .with_label_values(&[\"fail\"])\n                        .observe_duration(timer.stop_and_record());\n\n                    let qe = QualifiedEvent {\n                        original: event.clone(),\n                        qc_passed: false,\n                        failed_rule: Some(rule.name().to_string()),\n                    };\n                    self.publish(&qe, &self.cfg.output_fail_topic).await?;\n                    return Ok(());\n                }\n            }\n\n            // All rules passed\n            self.metrics\n                .processed\n                .with_label_values(&[\"pass\"])\n                .inc();\n            self.metrics\n                .latency\n                .with_label_values(&[\"pass\"])\n                .observe_duration(timer.stop_and_record());\n\n            let qe = QualifiedEvent {\n                original: event,\n                qc_passed: true,\n                failed_rule: None,\n            };\n            self.publish(&qe, &self.cfg.output_pass_topic).await?;\n            Ok(())\n        }\n        .instrument(span)\n        .await\n    }\n\n    async fn publish(&self, qe: &QualifiedEvent, topic: &str) -> Result<()> {\n        let payload =\n            serde_json::to_vec(qe).context(\"serializing QualifiedEvent for publishing\")?;\n        // The round-trip future resolves once acked by Kafka\n        let record = FutureRecord::to(topic)\n            .payload(&payload)\n            .key(&qe.original.id);\n        let _ = self\n            .producer\n            .send(record, Duration::from_secs(0))\n            .await\n            .map_err(|(e, _)| e)\n            .context(\"sending message to Kafka\")?;\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── Runner ───────────\n\n/// Build an async Kafka consumer committed on message success\nfn build_consumer(cfg: &Config) -> .set(\"enable.partition.eof\", \"false\")\n        .set(\"session.timeout.ms\", \"6000\")\n        .set(\"enable.auto.commit\", \"false\")\n        .set(\"enable.auto.offset.store\", \"false\")\n        .set(\"auto.offset.reset\", \"earliest\")\n        .create()?;\n    consumer.subscribe(&[&cfg.input_topic])?;\n    Ok(consumer)\n}\n\n/// Build a high-throughput producer (idempotency on)\nfn build_producer(cfg: &Config) -> Result<FutureProducer> {\n    let prod: FutureProducer = ClientConfig::new()\n        .set(\"bootstrap.servers\", &cfg.brokers)\n        .set(\"message.timeout.ms\", \"5000\")\n        .set(\"enable.idempotence\", \"true\")\n        .create()?;\n    Ok(prod)\n}\n\n/// Run QC service until Ctrl-C/TERM\npub async fn run() -> Result<()> {\n    // --- Logging / tracing\n    tracing_subscriber::fmt::init();\n\n    let cfg = Config::from_env();\n    info!(\"Launching QC microservice with config: {:?}\", cfg);\n\n    // Metrics\n    let metrics = Arc::new(Metrics::new());\n\n    // Kafka\n    let consumer = build_consumer(&cfg)?;\n    let producer = build_producer(&cfg)?;\n\n    // Quality controller\n    let qc = Arc::new(QualityController::new(cfg.clone(), producer, metrics.clone()));\n\n    // Graceful shutdown orchestration\n    let shutdown = Arc::new(Notify::new());\n    let shutdown_signal = shutdown.clone();\n\n    // Spawn Prometheus exporter\n    let metric_handle = {\n        let m = metrics.clone();\n        tokio::spawn(async move {\n            if let Err(e) = m.serve().await {\n                error!(\"Metrics exporter stopped: {e}\");\n            }\n        })\n    };\n\n    // Spawn Kafka loop\n    let kafka_handle = tokio::spawn(async move {\n        let mut stream = consumer.stream();\n        while let Some(msg) = stream.next().await {\n            match msg {\n                Ok(borrowed) => {\n                    if let Err(e) = qc.handle_message(borrowed.clone()).await {\n                        error!(\"QC error: {e}\");\n                    }\n                    // On success *or* failure, we commit offsets.\n                    if let Err(e) = consumer.store_offset_from_message(&borrowed) {\n                        error!(\"Failed to store offset: {e}\");\n                    }\n                }\n                Err(e) => {\n                    error!(\"Kafka error: {e}\");\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    });\n\n    // Listen for SIGINT / SIGTERM\n    tokio::spawn(async move {\n        let mut sig_int = signal(SignalKind::interrupt()).unwrap();\n        let mut sig_term = signal(SignalKind::terminate()).unwrap();\n        tokio::select! {\n            _ = sig_int.recv() => {\n                info!(\"Received SIGINT, shutting down\");\n            }\n            _ = sig_term.recv() => {\n                info!(\"Received SIGTERM, shutting down\");\n            }\n        }\n        shutdown_signal.notify_waiters();\n    });\n\n    // Wait until shutdown notified\n    shutdown.notified().await;\n    info!(\"Stopping QC microservice…\");\n\n    // Give the consumer some time to commit last offsets\n    consumer.commit_consumer_state(CommitMode::Sync)?;\n    consumer.unsubscribe();\n\n    kafka_handle.abort();\n    metric_handle.abort();\n\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n// Integration tests for utility components that power ChirpPulse’s\n     3→//! streaming/analytics internals.  Although these helpers live in\n     4→//! `chirp_pulse::utils`, re-implementations are provided here so the\n     5→//! tests are completely self-contained and compile in isolation.  In the\n     6→//! real project you would `use chirp_pulse::utils::*` instead.\n     7→//!\n     8→//! The utilities covered:\n     9→//! 1. WeightedMovingAverage – constant-time sliding window average\n    10→//! 2. RateLimitedExecutor     – client-side rate-limiter for burst control\n    11→//! 3. retry_async             – exponential back-off with jitter\n    12→//!\n    13→//! The tests exercise determinism, concurrency safety, and boundary\n    14→//! conditions (overflow, zero-division, etc.).\n    15→\n    16→use anyhow::{anyhow, bail, Result};\n    17→use rand::{rngs::StdRng, Rng, SeedableRng};\n    18→use std::{\n    19→    collections::VecDeque,\n    20→    sync::{Arc, Mutex},\n    21→    time::{Duration, Instant},\n    22→};\n    23→use tokio::{\n    24→    sync::Semaphore,\n    25→    task::JoinHandle,\n    26→    time::{sleep, timeout},\n    27→};\n    28→\n    29→/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n    30→/// WeightedMovingAverage\n    31→/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n    32→\n    33→#[derive(Debug)]\n    34→struct WeightedMovingAverage {\n    35→    /// Maximum number of samples to include in the window\n    36→    window: usize,\n    37→    /// (value, weight) pairs in order of insertion\n    38→    values: VecDeque<(f64, f64)>,\n    39→    /// Cached sum(value * weight) for O(1) updates\n    40→    sum_weighted: f64,\n    41→    /// Cached sum(weight) for O(1) updates\n    42→    sum_weights: f64,\n    43→}\n    44→\n    45→impl WeightedMovingAverage {\n    46→    fn new(window: usize) -> Self {\n    47→        assert!(window > 0, \"window must be greater than zero\");\n    48→        Self {\n    49→            window,\n    50→            values: VecDeque::with_capacity(window),\n    51→            sum_weighted: 0.0,\n    52→            sum_weights: 0.0,\n    53→        }\n    54→    }\n    55→\n    56→    /// Inserts a (value, weight) pair into the window.  If the window is\n    57→    /// full, the oldest entry is evicted.\n    58→    fn insert(&mut self, value: f64, weight: f64) {\n    59→        if weight <= 0.0 {\n    60→            // We tolerate zero‐weight samples (ignored) but negative\n    61→            // weights would break the math.\n    62→            panic!(\"weight must be positive\");\n    63→        }\n    64→\n    65→        // Push new sample\n    66→        self.values.push_back((value, weight));\n    67→        self.sum_weighted += value * weight;\n    68→        self.sum_weights += weight;\n    69→\n    70→        // Evict if over capacity\n    71→        if self.values.len() > self.window {\n    72→            if let Some((old_val, old_w)) = self.values.pop_front() {\n    73→                self.sum_weighted -= old_val * old_w;\n    74→                self.sum_weights -= old_w;\n    75→            }\n    76→        }\n    77→    }\n    78→\n    79→    fn average(&self) -> Option<f64> {\n    80→        if self.sum_weights == 0.0 {\n    81→            None\n    82→        } else {\n    83→            Some(self.sum_weighted / self.sum_weights)\n    84→        }\n    85→    }\n    86→\n    87→    fn len(&self) -> usize {\n    88→        self.values.len()\n    89→    }\n    90→\n    91→    fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {\n    92→        self.values.is_empty()\n    93→    }\n    94→}\n    95→\n    96→/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n    97→/// RateLimitedExecutor\n    98→/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n    99→\n   100→/// A simple, thread-safe token bucket that allows `capacity` “permits”\n   101→/// every `interval`.  Tasks acquire a permit before running; if none are\n   102→/// available they wait until the next refill.\n   103→#[derive(Debug)]\n   104→struct RateLimitedExecutor {\n   105→    capacity: usize,\n   106→    interval: Duration,\n   107→    /// Timestamps of used permits; size <= capacity\n   108→    permits: Mutex<VecDeque<Instant>>,\n   109→}\n   110→\n   111→impl RateLimitedExecutor {\n   112→    fn new(capacity: usize, interval: Duration) -> Self {\n   113→        assert!(capacity > 0, \"capacity must be > 0\");\n   114→        Self {\n   115→            capacity,\n   116→            interval,\n   117→            permits: Mutex::new(VecDeque::with_capacity(capacity)),\n   118→        }\n   119→    }\n   120→\n   121→    /// Runs the future once a permit is available.  The executor itself\n   122→    /// does not spawn; callers may `tokio::spawn` if needed.\n   123→    async fn execute<F, T>(&self, fut: F) -> T\n   124→    where\n   125→        F: std::future::Future<Output = T>,\n   126→    {\n   127→        loop {\n   128→            let mut guard = self.permits.lock().unwrap();\n   129→\n   130→            // Remove expired permits\n   131→            let now = Instant::now();\n   132→            while let Some(&front) = guard.front() {\n   133→                if now.duration_since(front) >= self.interval {\n   134→                    guard.pop_front();\n   135→                } else {\n   136→                    break;\n   137→                }\n   138→            }\n   139→\n   140→            if guard.len() < self.capacity {\n   141→                // Acquire token\n   142→                guard.push_back(now);\n   143→                drop(guard);\n   144→                break fut.await;\n   145→            }\n   146→\n   147→            // No permits.  Figure out when the next one expires.\n   148→            let wait_for = self\n   149→                .interval\n   150→                .checked_sub(now.duration_since(*guard.front().unwrap()))\n   151→                .unwrap_or(Duration::from_millis(1));\n   152→\n   153→            drop(guard); // Release lock before sleeping\n   154→            sleep(wait_for).await;\n   155→        }\n   156→    }\n   157→}\n   158→\n   159→/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n   160→/// retry_async – exponential back-off with jitter\n   161→/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n   162→\n   163→/// Configuration options for `retry_async`.\n   164→#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]\n   165→struct RetryConfig {\n   166→    max_retries: usize,\n   167→    base_delay: Duration,\n   168→    max_delay: Duration,\n   169→}\n   170→\n   171→/// Retries an async operation until it succeeds (Ok) or the max retry\n   172→/// count is reached.  Uses decorrelated jitter to avoid thundering herd.\n   173→async fn retry_async<F, Fut, T>(cfg: RetryConfig, mut factory: F) -> Result<T>\n   174→where\n   175→    F: FnMut(usize) -> Fut,\n   176→    Fut: std::future::Future<Output = Result<T>>,\n   177→{\n   178→    let mut attempt = 0;\n   179→    let rng_seed = 0xDEADBEEF; // deterministic for tests\n   180→    let mut rng = StdRng::seed_from_u64(rng_seed);\n   181→\n   182→    loop {\n   183→        match factory(attempt).await {\n   184→            Ok(v) => return Ok(v),\n   185→            Err(e) if attempt < cfg.max_retries => {\n   186→                attempt += 1;\n   187→\n   188→                // decorrelated jitter algorithm\n   189→                let exp_delay = cfg.base_delay * (1u32 << attempt as u32);\n   190→                let upper = std::cmp::min(exp_delay, cfg.max_delay);\n   191→                let sleep_for = Duration::from_millis(rng.gen_range(\n   192→                    cfg.base_delay.as_millis() as u64,\n   193→                    upper.as_millis() as u64 + 1,\n   194→                ));\n   195→                sleep(sleep_for).await;\n   196→\n   197→                // Continue retrying\n   198→                tracing::warn!(\n   199→                    attempt,\n   200→                    \"retrying after error: {e:?}, sleeping for {:?}\",\n   201→                    sleep_for\n   202→                );\n   203→            }\n   204→            Err(e) => {\n   205→                bail!(\"retry_async: exhausted all attempts: {e}\");\n   206→            }\n   207→        }\n   208→    }\n   209→}\n   210→\n   211→/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n   212→/// Tests\n   213→/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n   214→\n   215→#[test]\n   216→fn weighted_moving_average_basic_math() {\n   217→    let mut wma = 222→\n   223→    wma.insert(20.0, 1.0); // average = 15\n   224→    assert_eq!(wma.average().unwrap(), 15.0);\n   225→\n   226→    wma.insert(30.0, 2.0); // weighted (10*1 + 20*1 + 30*2) / 4 = 22.5\n   227→    assert_eq!(wma.average().unwrap(), 22.5);\n   228→\n   229→    // This insertion evicts the first sample (window = 3)\n   230→    wma.insert(0.0, 1.0); // (20*1 + 30*2 + 0*1) / 4 = 20\n   231→    assert_eq!(wma.average().unwrap(), 20.0);\n   232→    assert_eq!(wma.len(), 3);\n   233→}\n   234→\n   235→#[test]\n   236→fn weighted_moving_average_zero_weight_ignored() {\n   237→    let Integration tests for utility components that power ChirpPulse’s\n//! streaming/analytics internals.  Although these helpers live in\n//! `chirp_pulse::utils`, re-implementations are provided here so the\n//! tests are completely self-contained and compile in isolation.  In the\n//! real project you would `use chirp_pulse::utils::*` instead.\n//!\n//! The utilities covered:\n//! 1. WeightedMovingAverage – constant-time sliding window average\n//! 2. RateLimitedExecutor     – client-side rate-limiter for burst control\n//! 3. retry_async             – exponential back-off with jitter\n//!\n//! The tests exercise determinism, concurrency safety, and boundary\n//! conditions (overflow, zero-division, etc.).\n\nuse anyhow::{anyhow, bail, Result};\nuse rand::{rngs::StdRng, Rng, SeedableRng};\nuse std::{\n    collections::VecDeque,\n    sync::{Arc, Mutex},\n    time::{Duration, Instant},\n};\nuse tokio::{\n    sync::Semaphore,\n    task::JoinHandle,\n    time::{sleep, timeout},\n};\n\n/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n/// WeightedMovingAverage\n/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n#[derive(Debug)]\nstruct WeightedMovingAverage {\n    /// Maximum number of samples to include in the window\n    window: usize,\n    /// (value, weight) pairs in order of insertion\n    values: VecDeque<(f64, f64)>,\n    /// Cached sum(value * weight) for O(1) updates\n    sum_weighted: f64,\n    /// Cached sum(weight) for O(1) updates\n    sum_weights: f64,\n}\n\nimpl WeightedMovingAverage {\n    fn new(window: usize) -> Self {\n        assert!(window > 0, \"window must be greater than zero\");\n        Self {\n            window,\n            values: VecDeque::with_capacity(window),\n            sum_weighted: 0.0,\n            sum_weights: 0.0,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Inserts a (value, weight) pair into the window.  If the window is\n    /// full, the oldest entry is evicted.\n    fn insert(&mut self, value: f64, weight: f64) {\n        if weight <= 0.0 {\n            // We tolerate zero‐weight samples (ignored) but negative\n            // weights would break the math.\n            panic!(\"weight must be positive\");\n        }\n\n        // Push new sample\n        self.values.push_back((value, weight));\n        self.sum_weighted += value * weight;\n        self.sum_weights += weight;\n\n        // Evict if over capacity\n        if self.values.len() > self.window {\n            if let Some((old_val, old_w)) = self.values.pop_front() {\n                self.sum_weighted -= old_val * old_w;\n                self.sum_weights -= old_w;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    fn average(&self) -> Option<f64> {\n        if self.sum_weights == 0.0 {\n            None\n        } else {\n            Some(self.sum_weighted / self.sum_weights)\n        }\n    }\n\n    fn len(&self) -> usize {\n        self.values.len()\n    }\n\n    fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {\n        self.values.is_empty()\n    }\n}\n\n/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n/// RateLimitedExecutor\n/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n/// A simple, thread-safe token bucket that allows `capacity` “permits”\n/// every `interval`.  Tasks acquire a permit before running; if none are\n/// available they wait until the next refill.\n#[derive(Debug)]\nstruct RateLimitedExecutor {\n    capacity: usize,\n    interval: Duration,\n    /// Timestamps of used permits; size <= capacity\n    permits: Mutex<VecDeque<Instant>>,\n}\n\nimpl RateLimitedExecutor {\n    fn new(capacity: usize, interval: Duration) -> Self {\n        assert!(capacity > 0, \"capacity must be > 0\");\n        Self {\n            capacity,\n            interval,\n            permits: Mutex::new(VecDeque::with_capacity(capacity)),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Runs the future once a permit is available.  The executor itself\n    /// does not spawn; callers may `tokio::spawn` if needed.\n    async fn execute<F, T>(&self, fut: F) -> T\n    where\n        F: std::future::Future<Output = T>,\n    {\n        loop {\n            let mut guard = self.permits.lock().unwrap();\n\n            // Remove expired permits\n            let now = Instant::now();\n            while let Some(&front) = guard.front() {\n                if now.duration_since(front) >= self.interval {\n                    guard.pop_front();\n                } else {\n                    break;\n                }\n            }\n\n            if guard.len() < self.capacity {\n                // Acquire token\n                guard.push_back(now);\n                drop(guard);\n                break fut.await;\n            }\n\n            // No permits.  Figure out when the next one expires.\n            let wait_for = self\n                .interval\n                .checked_sub(now.duration_since(*guard.front().unwrap()))\n                .unwrap_or(Duration::from_millis(1));\n\n            drop(guard); // Release lock before sleeping\n            sleep(wait_for).await;\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n/// retry_async – exponential back-off with jitter\n/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n/// Configuration options for `retry_async`.\n#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]\nstruct RetryConfig {\n    max_retries: usize,\n    base_delay: Duration,\n    max_delay: Duration,\n}\n\n/// Retries an async operation until it succeeds (Ok) or the max retry\n/// count is reached.  Uses decorrelated jitter to avoid thundering herd.\nasync fn retry_async<F, Fut, T>(cfg: RetryConfig, mut factory: F) -> Result<T>\nwhere\n    F: FnMut(usize) -> Fut,\n    Fut: std::future::Future<Output = Result<T>>,\n{\n    let mut attempt = 0;\n    let rng_seed = 0xDEADBEEF; // deterministic for tests\n    let mut rng = StdRng::seed_from_u64(rng_seed);\n\n    loop {\n        match factory(attempt).await {\n            Ok(v) => return Ok(v),\n            Err(e) if attempt < cfg.max_retries => {\n                attempt += 1;\n\n                // decorrelated jitter algorithm\n                let exp_delay = cfg.base_delay * (1u32 << attempt as u32);\n                let upper = std::cmp::min(exp_delay, cfg.max_delay);\n                let sleep_for = Duration::from_millis(rng.gen_range(\n                    cfg.base_delay.as_millis() as u64,\n                    upper.as_millis() as u64 + 1,\n                ));\n                sleep(sleep_for).await;\n\n                // Continue retrying\n                tracing::warn!(\n                    attempt,\n                    \"retrying after error: {e:?}, sleeping for {:?}\",\n                    sleep_for\n                );\n            }\n            Err(e) => {\n                bail!(\"retry_async: exhausted all attempts: {e}\");\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n/// Tests\n/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n#[test]\nfn weighted_moving_average_basic_math() {\n    let mut wma = WeightedMovingAverage::new(3);\n\n    assert!(wma.average().is_none());\n    wma.insert(10.0, 1.0); // average = 10\n    assert_eq!(wma.average().unwrap(), 10.0);\n\n    wma.insert(20.0, 1.0); // average = 15\n    assert_eq!(wma.average().unwrap(), 15.0);\n\n    wma.insert(30.0, 2.0); // weighted (10*1 + 20*1 + 30*2) / 4 = 22.5\n    assert_eq!(wma.average().unwrap(), 22.5);\n\n    // This insertion evicts the first sample (window = 3)\n    wma.insert(0.0, 1.0); // (20*1 + 30*2 + 0*1) / 4 = 20\n    assert_eq!(wma.average().unwrap(), 20.0);\n    assert_eq!(wma.len(), 3);\n}\n\n#[test]\nfn weighted_moving_average_zero_weight_ignored() {\n    let mut wma = WeightedMovingAverage::new(4);\n\n    wma.insert(100.0, 0.0); // should panic? we chose to allow zero -> ignored\n    assert!(wma.average().is_none());\n\n    wma.insert(50.0, 5.0);\n    assert_eq!(wma.average(), Some(50.0));\n}\n\n#[tokio::test(flavor = \"multi_thread\", worker_threads = 4)]\nasync fn rate_limiter_blocks_until_permit_available() {\n    let rl = Arc::new(RateLimitedExecutor::new(2, Duration::from_millis(100)));\n\n    let started = Instant::now();\n    let mut handles: Vec<JoinHandle<Instant>> = vec![];\n\n    for _ in 0..4 {\n        let cloned = rl.clone();\n        handles.push(tokio::spawn(async move {\n            cloned\n                .execute(async {\n                    // trivial work\n                    Instant::now()\n                })\n                .await\n        }));\n    }\n\n    let results = futures::future::join_all(handles).await;\n    let mut timestamps: Vec<_> = results.into_iter().map(Result::unwrap).collect();\n    timestamps.sort();\n\n    // First two should execute immediately (< 20 ms)\n    assert!(timestamps[1].duration_since(started) < Duration::from_millis(20));\n\n    // Next two should be >= 100 ms after start (due to rate limit)\n    for ts in &timestamps[2..] {\n        assert!(ts.duration_since(started) >= Duration::from_millis(100));\n    }\n}\n\n#[tokio::test]\nasync fn retry_async_eventually_succeeds() -> Result<()> {\n    let cfg = RetryConfig {\n        max_retries: 5,\n        base_delay: Duration::from_millis(10),\n        max_delay: Duration::from_millis(100),\n    };\n\n    // Simulate a flaky function that fails 3 times then returns \"OK\"\n    let failures_before_success = Arc::new(Semaphore::new(3));\n\n    let result: String = retry_async(cfg, |attempt| {\n        let failures = failures_before_success.clone();\n        async move {\n            if failures.try_acquire().is_ok() {\n                Err(anyhow!(\"transient error on attempt {attempt}\"))\n            } else {\n                Ok(\"OK\".to_string())\n            }\n        }\n    })\n    .await?;\n\n    assert_eq!(&result, \"OK\");\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n#[tokio::test]\nasync fn retry_async_gives_up_after_max() {\n    let cfg = RetryConfig {\n        max_retries: 2,\n        base_delay: Duration::from_millis(1),\n        max_delay: Duration::from_millis(5),\n    };\n\n    let res = retry_async(cfg, |_| async { Err::<(), _>(anyhow!(\"always fails\")) }).await;\n\n    assert!(res.is_err(), \"should fail after exceeding max_retries\");\n}\n\n/// Make sure retry_async doesn't hang indefinitely when the wrapped\n/// future is cancellation-safe but never completes.  We protect against\n/// this with a global timeout.\n#[tokio::test]\nasync fn retry_async_respects_global_timeout() {\n    let cfg = RetryConfig {\n        max_retries: 3,\n        base_delay: Duration::from_millis(5),\n        max_delay: Duration::from_millis(15),\n    };\n\n    let hanging_future = || async {\n        // never returns\n        futures::future::pending::<Result<()>>().await\n    };\n\n    // If timeout triggers, the test passes\n    let timed = timeout(Duration::from_millis(100), retry_async(cfg, |_| hanging_future()));\n    assert!(timed.await.is_err(), \"global timeout should elapse\");\n}\n```","numLines":338,"startLine":1,"totalLines":338}}}

General-purpose utilities shared across ChirpPulse services.\n     5→//! Focus areas:\n     6→//!   • Robust async retry with exponential back-off + jitter\n     7→//!   • Task-spawn helpers with structured tracing\n     8→//!   • Lightweight timing/metrics wrappers\n     9→//!   • Misc. helpers (partition hashing, env access)\n    10→//!\n    11→//! These helpers allow individual micro-services to remain lean while still\n    12→//! benefiting from consistent observability and failure-handling semantics.\n    13→\n    14→use rand::{thread_rng, Rng};\n    15→use std::{\n    16→    future::Future,\n    17→    hash::Hasher,\n    18→    pin::Pin,\n    19→    time::{Duration, Instant},\n    20→};\n    21→use tokio::{task::JoinHandle, time::sleep};\n    22→use tracing::{error, instrument, warn};\n    23→\n    24→/// Re-export a boxed error to simplify generic signatures within the\n    25→/// application. We rely on `Send + Sync` so values can freely cross\n    26→/// thread/async boundaries.\n    27→pub type BoxError = Box<dyn std::error::Error + Send + Sync + 'static>;\n    28→\n    29→/// Unified error type for the `utils` module.\n    30→///\n    31→/// Use domain-specific errors in your service crates; convert to/from\n    32→/// `UtilError` when necessary (e.g. for back-pressure signalling).\n    33→#[derive(thiserror::Error, Debug)]\n    34→pub enum UtilError {\n    35→    /// All attempts have been consumed without success.\n    36→    #[error(\"retries exhausted after {0} attempts\")]\n    37→    RetriesExhausted(usize),\n    38→}\n    39→\n    40→/// Default maximum retry attempts when the caller supplies `0`.\n    41→const DEFAULT_MAX_ATTEMPTS: usize = 5;\n    42→\n    43→/// Attempt an asynchronous operation multiple times with exponential back-off\n    44→/// and ±20 % jitter.\n    45→///\n    46→/// • `operation` is an async closure producing `Result<T, E>`.  \n    47→/// • `max_attempts == 0` triggers [`DEFAULT_MAX_ATTEMPTS`].  \n    48→/// • `base_delay` is the initial sleep duration; it doubles each attempt.  \n    49→///\n    50→/// All errors are mapped to [`BoxError`] so we can treat them uniformly.\n    51→///\n    52→/// Example\n    53→/// -------\n    54→/// ```rust,no_run\n    55→/// use chirp_pulse_utils::{retry_async, BoxError};\n    56→/// use std::time::Duration;\n    57→///\n    58→/// async fn fetch() -> Result<(), BoxError> {\n    59→///     // ... call a flaky upstream service ...\n    60→/// #   Ok(())\n    61→/// }\n    62→///\n    63→/// #[tokio::main]\n    64→/// async fn main() -> Result<(), BoxError> {\n    65→///     retry_async(\n    66→///         || async { fetch().await },\n    67→///         4,\n    68→///         Duration::from_millis(100),\n    69→///     )\n    70→///     .await?;\n    71→///     Ok(())\n    72→/// }\n    73→/// ```\n    74→#[instrument(level = \"trace\", skip(operation))]\n    75→pub async fn retry_async<F, Fut, T>(\n    76→    mut operation: F,\n    77→    max_attempts: usize,\n    78→    base_delay: Duration,\n    79→) -> Result<T, UtilError>\n    80→where\n    81→    F: FnMut() -> Fut + Send + 'static,\n    82→    Fut: Future<Output = Result<T, BoxError>> + Send,\n    83→    T: Send + 'static,\n    84→{\n    85→    let max_attempts = if max_attempts == 0 {\n    86→        DEFAULT_MAX_ATTEMPTS\n    87→    } else {\n    88→        max_attempts\n    89→    };\n    90→\n    91→    let mut attempt = 0usize;\n    92→    loop {\n    93→        attempt += 1;\n    94→\n    95→        match operation().await {\n    96→            Ok(val) => return Ok(val),\n    97→            Err(err) => {\n    98→                if attempt >= max_attempts {\n    99→                    error!(attempt, error = %err, \"operation failed; retry limit reached\");\n   100→                    return Err(UtilError::RetriesExhausted(attempt));\n   101→                }\n   102→\n   103→                // Calc delay = base * 2^(attempt-1) ±20 % jitter\n   104→                let exp_delay = base_delay * 2_u32.pow((attempt - 1) as u32);\n   105→                let jitter_factor = thread_rng().gen_range(0.8..=1.2);\n   106→                let sleep_for = exp_delay.mul_f32(jitter_factor as f32);\n   107→\n   108→                warn!(\n   109→                    attempt,\n   110→                    max_attempts,\n   111→                    error = %err,\n   112→                    retry_in_ms = sleep_for.as_millis(),\n   113→                    \"operation failed; retrying…\"\n   114→                );\n   115→\n   116→                sleep(sleep_for).await;\n   117→            }\n   118→        }\n   119→    }\n   120→}\n   121→\n   122→/// Spawn a Tokio task with a meaningful name and automatic error logging.\n   123→///\n   124→/// Panics inside the task are propagated to the parent runtime (Tokio\n   125→/// already aborts by default). _Error_ values returned from the task are\n   126→/// logged but do **not** bubble up, preventing accidental unawaited\n   127→/// `JoinHandle` leaks.\n   128→///\n   129→/// Returns the [`JoinHandle`] so callers may `.await`/`.abort()` as needed.\n   130→///\n   131→/// ```rust,no_run\n   132→/// use chirp_pulse_utils::{spawn_named_task, BoxError};\n   133→///\n   134→/// async fn job() -> Result<(), BoxError> {\n   135→///     // ... do work ...\n   136→/// #   Ok(())\n   137→/// }\n   138→///\n   139→/// let handle = spawn_named_task(\"my_background_job\", job());\n   140→/// ```\n   141→pub fn spawn_named_task<F>(name: &'static str, fut: F) -> JoinHandle<()>\n   142→where\n   143→    F: Future<Output = Result<(), BoxError>> + Send + 'static,\n   144→{\n   145→    tokio::task::Builder::new()\n   146→        .name(name)\n   147→        .spawn(async move {\n   148→            if let Err(err) = fut.await {\n   149→                error!(task = name, error = %err, \"task exited with error\");\n   150→            }\n   151→        })\n   152→        .expect(\"failed to spawn task\")\n   153→}\n   154→\n   155→/// Time an async operation and submit the duration as a histogram metric.\n   156→///\n   157→/// Even if `metrics` is disabled at compile-time, the no-op macro\n   158→/// ensures zero runtime cost.\n   159→///\n   160→/// ```rust,no_run\n   161→/// use chirp_pulse_utils::measure_time_async;\n   162→///\n   163→/// # async fn compute() {}\n   164→/// let _ = measure_time_async(\"my_op.latency_seconds\", || async { compute().await }).await;\n   165→/// ```\n   166→#[instrument(level = \"trace\", skip(operation))]\n   167→pub async fn measure_time_async<F, Fut, T>(metric_name: &'static str, operation: F) -> T\n   168→where\n   169→    F: FnOnce() -> Fut,\n   170→    Fut: Future<Output = T>,\n   171→{\n   172→    let start = Instant::now();\n   173→    let res = operation().await;\n   174→    let elapsed = start.elapsed();\n   175→    metrics::histogram!(metric_name, elapsed.as_secs_f64());\n   176→    res\n   177→}\n   178→\n   179→/// Retrieve the value of an environment variable or panic with a descriptive\n   180→/// message. Suitable for required configuration keys that should fail fast\n   181→/// during service boot.\n   182→pub fn env_var(key: &str) -> String {\n   183→    match std::env::var(key) {\n   184→        Ok(v) => v,\n   185→        Err(_) => {\n   186→            let msg = format!(\"environment variable `{key}` must be set\");\n   187→            error!(\"{msg}\");\n   188→            panic!(\"{msg}\");\n   189→        }\n   190→    }\n   191→}\n   192→\n   193→/// Compute a stable 64-bit partition key for the given input string.\n   194→///\n   195→/// We use FNV-1a (fast, non-cryptographic) so identical inputs always hash to\n   196→/// the same value, enabling event-ordering guarantees when producing to\n   197→/// Kafka/Kinesis.\n   198→///\n   199→/// Example:\n   200→/// ```\n   201→/// use chirp_pulse_utils::partition_key_from_str;\n   202→/// let key = partition_key_from_str(\"@elonmusk\");\n   203→/// ```\n   204→pub fn partition_key_from_str(input: &str) -> u64 {\n   205→    let mut hasher = fnv::FnvHasher::default();\n   206→    hasher.write(input.as_bytes());\n   207→    hasher.finish()\n   208→}\n   209→\n   210→/////////////////////////////\n   211→//           Tests         //\n   212→/////////////////////////////\n   213→\n   214→#[cfg(test)]\n   215→mod tests {\n   216→    use super::*;\n   217→    use tokio::runtime::Runtime;\n   218→\n General-purpose utilities shared across ChirpPulse services.\n//! Focus areas:\n//!   • Robust async retry with exponential back-off + jitter\n//!   • Task-spawn helpers with structured tracing\n//!   • Lightweight timing/metrics wrappers\n//!   • Misc. helpers (partition hashing, env access)\n//!\n//! These helpers allow individual micro-services to remain lean while still\n//! benefiting from consistent observability and failure-handling semantics.\n\nuse rand::{thread_rng, Rng};\nuse std::{\n    future::Future,\n    hash::Hasher,\n    pin::Pin,\n    time::{Duration, Instant},\n};\nuse tokio::{task::JoinHandle, time::sleep};\nuse tracing::{error, instrument, warn};\n\n/// Re-export a boxed error to simplify generic signatures within the\n/// application. We rely on `Send + Sync` so values can freely cross\n/// thread/async boundaries.\npub type BoxError = Box<dyn std::error::Error + Send + Sync + 'static>;\n\n/// Unified error type for the `utils` module.\n///\n/// Use domain-specific errors in your service crates; convert to/from\n/// `UtilError` when necessary (e.g. for back-pressure signalling).\n#[derive(thiserror::Error, Debug)]\npub enum UtilError {\n    /// All attempts have been consumed without success.\n    #[error(\"retries exhausted after {0} attempts\")]\n    RetriesExhausted(usize),\n}\n\n/// Default maximum retry attempts when the caller supplies `0`.\nconst DEFAULT_MAX_ATTEMPTS: usize = 5;\n\n/// Attempt an asynchronous operation multiple times with exponential back-off\n/// and ±20 % jitter.\n///\n/// • `operation` is an async closure producing `Result<T, E>`.  \n/// • `max_attempts == 0` triggers [`DEFAULT_MAX_ATTEMPTS`].  \n/// • `base_delay` is the initial sleep duration; it doubles each attempt.  \n///\n/// All errors are mapped to [`BoxError`] so we can treat them uniformly.\n///\n/// Example\n/// -------\n/// ```rust,no_run\n/// use chirp_pulse_utils::{retry_async, BoxError};\n/// use std::time::Duration;\n///\n/// async fn fetch() -> Result<(), BoxError> {\n///     // ... call a flaky upstream service ...\n/// #   Ok(())\n/// }\n///\n/// #[tokio::main]\n/// async fn main() -> Result<(), BoxError> {\n///     retry_async(\n///         || async { fetch().await },\n///         4,\n///         Duration::from_millis(100),\n///     )\n///     .await?;\n///     Ok(())\n/// }\n/// ```\n#[instrument(level = \"trace\", skip(operation))]\npub async fn retry_async<F, Fut, T>(\n    mut operation: F,\n    max_attempts: usize,\n    base_delay: Duration,\n) -> Result<T, UtilError>\nwhere\n    F: FnMut() -> Fut + Send + 'static,\n    Fut: Future<Output = Result<T, BoxError>> + Send,\n    T: Send + 'static,\n{\n    let max_attempts = if max_attempts == 0 {\n        DEFAULT_MAX_ATTEMPTS\n    } else {\n        max_attempts\n    };\n\n    let mut attempt = 0usize;\n    loop {\n        attempt += 1;\n\n        match operation().await {\n            Ok(val) => return Ok(val),\n            Err(err) => {\n                if attempt >= max_attempts {\n                    error!(attempt, error = %err, \"operation failed; retry limit reached\");\n                    return Err(UtilError::RetriesExhausted(attempt));\n                }\n\n                // Calc delay = base * 2^(attempt-1) ±20 % jitter\n                let exp_delay = base_delay * 2_u32.pow((attempt - 1) as u32);\n                let jitter_factor = thread_rng().gen_range(0.8..=1.2);\n                let sleep_for = exp_delay.mul_f32(jitter_factor as f32);\n\n                warn!(\n                    attempt,\n                    max_attempts,\n                    error = %err,\n                    retry_in_ms = sleep_for.as_millis(),\n                    \"operation failed; retrying…\"\n                );\n\n                sleep(sleep_for).await;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Spawn a Tokio task with a meaningful name and automatic error logging.\n///\n/// Panics inside the task are propagated to the parent runtime (Tokio\n/// already aborts by default). _Error_ values returned from the task are\n/// logged but do **not** bubble up, preventing accidental unawaited\n/// `JoinHandle` leaks.\n///\n/// Returns the [`JoinHandle`] so callers may `.await`/`.abort()` as needed.\n///\n/// ```rust,no_run\n/// use chirp_pulse_utils::{spawn_named_task, BoxError};\n///\n/// async fn job() -> Result<(), BoxError> {\n///     // ... do work ...\n/// #   Ok(())\n/// }\n///\n/// let handle = spawn_named_task(\"my_background_job\", job());\n/// ```\npub fn spawn_named_task<F>(name: &'static str, fut: F) -> JoinHandle<()>\nwhere\n    F: Future<Output = Result<(), BoxError>> + Send + 'static,\n{\n    tokio::task::Builder::new()\n        .name(name)\n        .spawn(async move {\n            if let Err(err) = fut.await {\n                error!(task = name, error = %err, \"task exited with error\");\n            }\n        })\n        .expect(\"failed to spawn task\")\n}\n\n/// Time an async operation and submit the duration as a histogram metric.\n///\n/// Even if `metrics` is disabled at compile-time, the no-op macro\n/// ensures zero runtime cost.\n///\n/// ```rust,no_run\n/// use chirp_pulse_utils::measure_time_async;\n///\n/// # async fn compute() {}\n/// let _ = measure_time_async(\"my_op.latency_seconds\", || async { compute().await }).await;\n/// ```\n#[instrument(level = \"trace\", skip(operation))]\npub async fn measure_time_async<F, Fut, T>(metric_name: &'static str, operation: F) -> T\nwhere\n    F: FnOnce() -> Fut,\n    Fut: Future<Output = T>,\n{\n    let start = Instant::now();\n    let res = operation().await;\n    let elapsed = start.elapsed();\n    metrics::histogram!(metric_name, elapsed.as_secs_f64());\n    res\n}\n\n/// Retrieve the value of an environment variable or panic with a descriptive\n/// message. Suitable for required configuration keys that should fail fast\n/// during service boot.\npub fn env_var(key: &str) -> String {\n    match std::env::var(key) {\n        Ok(v) => v,\n        Err(_) => {\n            let msg = format!(\"environment variable `{key}` must be set\");\n            error!(\"{msg}\");\n            panic!(\"{msg}\");\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Compute a stable 64-bit partition key for the given input string.\n///\n/// We use FNV-1a (fast, non-cryptographic) so identical inputs always hash to\n/// the same value, enabling event-ordering guarantees when producing to\n/// Kafka/Kinesis.\n///\n/// Example:\n/// ```\n/// use chirp_pulse_utils::partition_key_from_str;\n/// let key = partition_key_from_str(\"@elonmusk\");\n/// ```\npub fn partition_key_from_str(input: &str) -> u64 {\n    let mut hasher = fnv::FnvHasher::default();\n    hasher.write(input.as_bytes());\n    hasher.finish()\n}\n\n/////////////////////////////\n// **module_79.txt**: Contains the Kafka consumer wrapper (`kafka_event_stream`) and event deserialization logic (`deserialize_event`). The `QualityPipeline` struct directly creates and manages the Kafka connection.\n\n2. **module_41.txt**: The `QualityController` handles data quality rules and is instantiated via the `run()` function which builds Kafka consumer/producer directly using `build_consumer()` and `build_producer()`.\n\n3. **Coupling Issues**:\n   - Data source creation is embedded in the orchestrator functions\n   - No abstraction layer for different data sources\n   - Testing requires mock Kafka infrastructure\n   - Adding new sources (Reddit, Mastodon) would require significant code changes\n\n### Architectural Patterns Used\n\n- **Pipeline Pattern**: Used in `QualityPipeline` for sequential processing of events\n- **Strategy Pattern**: `QualityRule` trait enables swappable validation rules\n- **Observer Pattern**: Metrics are emitted via Prometheus for observability\n- **Dependency Injection**: Will be introduced for `DataSource` abstraction\n\n### Refactoring Approach\n\nThe refactoring introduces a new `DataSource` trait abstraction layer that:\n1. Decouples the processing pipeline from specific data source implementations\n2. Enables easy addition of new data sources\n3. Facilitates testing with mock data sources\n4. Preserves the existing functionality in `LegacyStreamSource`\n\n## Implementation\n\n### Files to Create\n\n#### 1. src/data_sources/mod.rs (NEW)\n\n```rust\n// File: /app/project/src/data_sources/mod.rs\n// Purpose: Define the DataSource trait abstraction\n\n//! Data Source Abstraction Layer for ChirpPulse\n//!\n//! This module defines the `DataSource` trait, which provides a uniform interface\n//! for connecting to various data sources and yielding streams of raw data objects.\n//! This abstraction enables the ChirpPulse system to support multiple data providers\n//!  self\n    }\n\n    pub fn with_topic(mut self, topic: impl Into<String>) -> Self {\n        self.topic = Some(topic.into());\n        self\n    }\n\n    pub fn with_buffer_size(mut self, size: usize) \"LegacyStreamSource consumer created\"\n        );\n\n        Ok(consumer)\n    }\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl DataSource for LegacyStreamSource {\n    fn name(&self) -> &'static str {\n        \"legacy_kafka\"\n    }\n\n    async fn stream(\n        &self,\n        config: &SourceConfig,\n    ) -> Pin<Box<dyn Stream<Item = Result<serde_json::Value, anyhow::Error>> + Send>> {\n        // Build the consumer\n        let consumer = match self.build_consumer(config) {\n            Ok(c) => Arc::new(c),\n            Err(e) => {\n                error!(error = %e, \"Failed to build Kafka consumer\");\n                return Box::pin(futures::stream::once(async move {\n                    Err(anyhow::anyhow!(\"Failed to create consumer: {}\", e))\n                }));\n            }\n        };\n\n        // Create an async stream from the Kafka consumer\n        let stream = async_stream::stream! {\n            let consumer = consumer.clone();\n            let mut message_stream = consumer.stream();\n\n            while let Some(result) = message_stream.next().await {\n                match result {\n                    Ok(borrowed_msg) => {\n                        match Self::deserialize_message(&borrowed_msg) {\n                            Ok(value) => yield Ok(value),\n                            Err(e) => {\n                                warn!(error = %e, \"Failed to deserialize message\");\n                                yield Err(e);\n                            }\n                        }\n                    }\n                    Err(e) => {\n                        error!(error = %e, \"Kafka consumer error\");\n                        yield Err(anyhow::anyhow!(\"Kafka error: {}\", e));\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n        };\n\n        Box::pin(stream)\n    }\n\n    async fn health_check(&self, config: &SourceConfig) -> Result<(), anyhow::Error> {\n        let consumer = self.build_consumer(config)?;\n        // Try to fetch metadata to verify connection\n        let timeout = Duration::from_millis(config.timeout_ms);\n        consumer\n            .fetch_metadata(config.topic.as_deref(), timeout)\n            .context(\"Failed to fetch Kafka metadata\")?;\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\nimpl LegacyStreamSource {\n    /// Deserialize a Kafka message into a JSON Value.\n    fn deserialize_message(msg: &BorrowedMessage<'_>) -> Result<serde_json::Value, anyhow::Error> {\n        let payload = msg\n            .payload()\n            .ok_or_else(|| anyhow::anyhow!(\"Message has no payload\"))?;\n\n        serde_json::from_slice(payload)\n            .context(\"Failed to deserialize message as JSON\")\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_legacy_source_builder() {\n        let source = LegacyStreamSource::new()\n            .with_sasl_auth(\"user\", \"pass\")\n            .with_security_protocol(\"SASL_SSL\");\n\n        assert_eq!(source.sasl_username, Some(\"user\".to_string()));\n        assert_eq!(source.sasl_password, Some(\"pass\".to_string()));\n        assert_eq!(source.security_protocol, \"SASL_SSL\");\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_default_legacy_source() {\n        let source = LegacyStreamSource::default();\n        assert_eq!(source.sasl_username, None);\n        assert_eq!(source.security_protocol, \"PLAINTEXT\");\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Files to Modify\n\n#### 3. Lines: 259-279\n\n/// Central controller running all QC rules\npub struct QualityController {\n    rules: Vec<DynRule>,\n    metrics: Arc<Metrics>,\n    producer: FutureProducer,\n    cfg: Box::new(LanguagePresentRule),\n            Box::new(TimestampNotFutureRule),\n            Box::new(SentimentBoundsRule),\n        ];\n        Self {\n            rules,\n            metrics,\n **module_79.txt**: Contains the Kafka consumer wrapper (`kafka_event_stream`) and event deserialization logic (`deserialize_event`). The `QualityPipeline` struct directly creates and manages the Kafka connection.\n\n2. **module_41.txt**: The `QualityController` handles data quality rules and is instantiated via the `run()` function which builds Kafka consumer/producer directly using `build_consumer()` and `build_producer()`.\n\n3. **Coupling Issues**:\n   - Data source creation is embedded in the orchestrator functions\n   - No abstraction layer for different data sources\n   - Testing requires mock Kafka infrastructure\n   - Adding new sources (Reddit, Mastodon) would require significant code changes\n\n### Architectural Patterns Used\n\n- **Pipeline Pattern**: Used in `QualityPipeline` for sequential processing of events\n- **Strategy Pattern**: `QualityRule` trait enables swappable validation rules\n- **Observer Pattern**: Metrics are emitted via Prometheus for observability\n- **Dependency Injection**: Will be introduced for `DataSource` abstraction\n\n### Refactoring Approach\n\nThe refactoring introduces a new `DataSource` trait abstraction layer that:\n1. Decouples the processing pipeline from specific data source implementations\n2. Enables easy addition of new data sources\n3. Facilitates testing with mock data sources\n4. Preserves the existing functionality in `LegacyStreamSource`\n\n## Implementation\n\n### Files to Create\n\n#### 1. src/data_sources/mod.rs (NEW)\n\n```rust\n// File: /app/project/src/data_sources/mod.rs\n// Purpose: Define the DataSource trait abstraction\n\n//! Data Source Abstraction Layer for ChirpPulse\n//!\n//! This module defines the `DataSource` trait, which provides a uniform interface\n//! for connecting to various data sources and yielding streams of raw data objects.\n//! This abstraction enables the ChirpPulse system to support multiple data providers\n//! (e.g., Twitter, Reddit, Mastodon) without tight coupling in the core processing\n//! pipeline.\n\npub mod legacy_source;\n\nuse std::pin::Pin;\n\nuse async_trait::async_trait;\nuse futures::Stream;\nuse serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};\n\n/// Configuration parameters for connecting to a data source.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct SourceConfig {\n /app/project/src/data_sources/legacy_source.rs\n// Purpose: Implement LegacyStreamSource wrapping existing Kafka logic\n\n//! Legacy Stream Source Implementation\n//!\n//! This module wraps the original Kafka-based data ingestion logic in the\n//! DataSource trait interface, enabling backward compatibility while\n//! supporting the new abstraction layer.\n\nuse std::pin::Pin;\nuse std::sync::Arc;\nuse std::time::Duration;\n\nuse anyhow::{Context, Result};\nuse async_trait::async_trait;\nuse futures::{Stream, StreamExt};\nuse rdkafka::consumer::{Consumer, StreamConsumer};\nuse rdkafka::message::{BorrowedMessage, Message};\nuse rdkafka::ClientConfig;\nuse serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};\nuse tracing::{error, info, warn};\n\nuse super::{DataSource, SourceConfig};\n\n/// The original Kafka-based data source implementation.\n///\n/// This struct encapsulates all the legacy connection and streaming logic\n/// that was previously spread across module_79 and module_41, providing\n/// a unified interface through the DataSource trait.\npub struct LegacyStreamSource {\n    /// SASL username for Kafka authentication\n    sasl_username: Option<String>,\n    /// SASL password for Kafka authentication\n    sasl_password: Option<String>,\n    /// Security protocol (PLAINTEXT, SSL, SASL_SSL, etc.)\n    security_protocol: String,\n}\n\nimpl Default for LegacyStreamSource {\n    fn default() -> Self {\n        Self {\n            sasl_username: None,\n            sasl_password: None,\n            security_protocol: \"PLAINTEXT\".to_string(),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl LegacyStreamSource {\n    /// Create a new LegacyStreamSource with default settings.\n    pub fn new() -> Self {\n        Self::default()\n    }\n\n    /// Configure SASL authentication.\n    pub fn with_sasl_auth(\n        mut self,\n        username: impl Into<String>,\n        password: impl Into<String>,\n    ) -> Self {\n        self.sasl_username = Some(username.into());\n        self.sasl_password = Some(password.into());\n        self.security_protocol = \"SASL_SSL\".to_string();\n        self\n    }\n\n    /// Set the security protocol.\n    pub fn with_security_protocol(mut self, protocol: impl Into<String>) -> Self {\n        self.security_protocol = protocol.into();\n        self\n    }\n\n    /// Build a Kafka StreamConsumer from the provided configuration.\n    fn build_consumer(&self, config: &SourceConfig) -> Result<StreamConsumer> {\n        let mut client_config = ClientConfig::new();\n\n        client_config\n            .set(\"bootstrap.servers\", &config.endpoint)\n            .set(\"group.id\", config.consumer_group.as_deref().unwrap_or(\"chirp-pulse\"))\n            .set(\"enable.partition.eof\", \"false\")\n            .set(\"session.timeout.ms\", \"6000\")\n            .set(\"enable.auto.commit\", \"false\")\n            .set(\"enable.auto.offset.store\", \"false\")\n            .set(\"auto.offset.reset\", \"earliest\")\n            .set(\"security.protocol\", &self.security_protocol);\n\n        if let (Some(username), Some(password)) = (&self.sasl_username, &self.sasl_password) {\n            client_config\n                .set(\"sasl.mechanism\", \"SCRAM-SHA-512\")\n                .set(\"sasl.username\", username)\n                .set(\"sasl.password\", password);\n        }\n\n        let consumer: StreamConsumer = client_config\n            .create()\n            .context(\"Failed to create Kafka consumer\")?;\n\n        if let Some(topic) = &config.topic {\n            consumer.subscribe(&[topic])?;\n        }\n\n        info!(\n            source_id = %config.source_id,\n            endpoint = %config.endpoint,\n            topic = ?config.topic,\n            \"LegacyStreamSource consumer created\"\n        );\n\n        Ok(consumer)\n    }\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl DataSource for LegacyStreamSource {\n    fn name(&self) -> &'static str {\n        \"legacy_kafka\"\n    }\n\n    async fn stream(\n        &self,\n        config: &SourceConfig,\n    ) -> Pin<Box<dyn Stream<Item = Result<serde_json::Value, anyhow::Error>> + Send>> {\n        // Build the consumer\n        let consumer = match self.build_consumer(config) {\n            Ok(c) => Arc::new(c),\n            Err(e) => {\n                error!(error = %e, \"Failed to build Kafka consumer\");\n                return Box::pin(futures::stream::once(async move {\n                    Err(anyhow::anyhow!(\"Failed to create consumer: {}\", e))\n                }));\n            }\n        };\n\n        // Create an async stream from the Kafka consumer\n        let stream = async_stream::stream! {\n            let consumer = consumer.clone();\n            let mut message_stream = consumer.stream();\n\n            while let Some(result) = message_stream.next().await {\n                match result {\n                    Ok(borrowed_msg) => {\n                        match Self::deserialize_message(&borrowed_msg) {\n                            Ok(value) => yield Ok(value),\n                            Err(e) => {\n                                warn!(error = %e, \"Failed to deserialize message\");\n                                yield Err(e);\n                            }\n                        }\n                    }\n                    Err(e) => {\n                        error!(error = %e, \"Kafka consumer error\");\n                        yield Err(anyhow::anyhow!(\"Kafka error: {}\", e));\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n        };\n\n        Box::pin(stream)\n    }\n\n    async fn health_check(&self, config: &SourceConfig) -> Result<(), anyhow::Error> {\n        let consumer = self.build_consumer(config)?;\n        // Try to fetch metadata to verify connection\n        let timeout = Duration::from_millis(config.timeout_ms);\n        consumer\n            .fetch_metadata(config.topic.as_deref(), timeout)\n            .context(\"Failed to fetch Kafka metadata\")?;\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\nimpl LegacyStreamSource {\n    /// Deserialize a Kafka message into a JSON Value.\n    fn deserialize_message(msg: &BorrowedMessage<'_>) -> Result<serde_json::Value, anyhow::Error> {\n        let payload = msg\n            .payload()\n            .ok_or_else(|| anyhow::anyhow!(\"Message has no payload\"))?;\n\n        serde_json::from_slice(payload)\n            .context(\"Failed to deserialize message as JSON\")\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_legacy_source_builder() {\n        let source = LegacyStreamSource::new()\n            .with_sasl_auth(\"user\", \"pass\")\n            .with_security_protocol(\"SASL_SSL\");\n\n        assert_eq!(source.sasl_username, Some(\"user\".to_string()));\n        assert_eq!(source.sasl_password, Some(\"pass\".to_string()));\n        assert_eq!(source.security_protocol, \"SASL_SSL\");\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_default_legacy_source() {\n        let source = LegacyStreamSource::default();\n        assert_eq!(source.sasl_username, None);\n        assert_eq!(source.security_protocol, \"PLAINTEXT\");\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Files to Modify\n\n#### 3.
